                                               FOREWORD

         The agriculture sector is considered as key in enhancing the economic growth of a
country along with mitigating the concerns of food security. This sector is the main source of
rural poverty alleviation and employment generation. Agriculture sector, contributing 22.9
percent in GDP and 37.4 percent in employment generation, ensures food security and provide
raw material to the industrial sector. It is the main sector for social formation in our country.

         Statistical data and information are crucial in attaining the national development goals
and socio-economic development. Adequate, reliable, and accurate statistical data is required
in economic planning, and analyzing the data facilitates in acquiring results of the present
economic status and also in explicating the future outcomes. It provides the basis for the factors
that shape development planning and decisions at the regional and district levels.

         The publication "Crops Area & Production (Districts Wise)" comprises of latest
district-wise data of Major and Minor crops including Food and Cash crops, Pulses and
Oilseeds, Fruits, Vegetables, and Condiments statistics for the years 2021-22 and 2022-23.
This publication will prove to be immensely helpful in facilitating the research work and will
help government functionaries, academicians, and all those who are interested in the issues
relating to the development of the Agriculture Sector.

         I wish to appreciate the efforts made by the team who are directly or indirectly involved
in the compilation of this publication, including the federal and provincial organizations, which
positively assisted the Economic Wing to compile the publications.

         Suggestions and comments for further improvement of this publication would highly
be appreciated.

                         (Capt. (Retd.) Muhammad Asif)
                                        Secretary

Islamabad, Feb 19, 2024
                                           Executive Summary

         Pakistan, a predominantly agrarian economy, relies significantly on its agriculture
sector. With a rich agricultural history dating back centuries, agriculture remains the backbone
of Pakistan's economy, contributing 22.9 percent in GDP, 37.4 percent in employment, and
export earnings. During Fiscal year 2022-23, the contribution of important crops recorded at
18.23 percent to value addition in agriculture sector and 4.18 percent to GDP. Other crops
contributed 14.49 percent in value addition of agriculture sector and 3.32 percent in GDP.

The sector mainly comprises of crops, livestock, and fisheries, with wheat, cotton, rice, maize
and sugarcane being the major crops. Agriculture is highly dependent on the country's irrigation
system, as the majority of the land is arid or semi-arid. In recent years, the sector has faced
numerous challenges, such as water scarcity, climate change, and low productivity, which have
negatively impacted the country's food security and economic growth. However, the
government has implemented various initiatives, such as the Kissan package, Productivity
Enhancement program for Wheat, Sugarcane, Oilseeds and Pulses which has significantly
contributed in improving the sector's performance and address these challenges. Despite the
obstacles, agriculture remains a vital sector for Pakistan's development, and the country has the
potential to become a major food exporter in the region with the right policies and investments.

Agriculture supports industry and earns foreign exchange through exports and reduces poverty
at national level. The development of agriculture depends on sound policies and plan based on
the empirical data. Ministry of National Food Security and Research has prepared its
publication "Crops Area & Production (District wise) 2022-23". This publication is enriched
with the data of 51 crops on the district level with indicators including area and production to
facilitate the policy makers, researchers, academia and students dealing with Agriculture.

The production of major crops during 2022-23, such as wheat and maize has indicated
considerable improvement compared to last year and has surpassed the production targets. On
contrast, sugarcane, rice and cotton has indicated a decrease compared to last year. The details
of the major crops are explained below:
Wheat:

         Wheat is a major Rabi crop of the country. The production target was 28.37 million
tons. The wheat production during 2022-23 was estimated at 28.16 million tonnes against 26.2
million tonnes last year. The percentage change of production as compared to last year is 7.45
percent. During 2022-23, area sown increased to 9 million hectares (0.62 percent) against last
years of 8.9 million hectares. Wheat production has increased due to good weather condition
and timely sowing. The minimum support price in Punjab was 4000 Rs/40 Kgs whereas Sindh
announced the minimum support price at Rs 4100/40 Kg

Rice:

         Rice is an important food as well as cash crop. It is the second main staple food crop
after wheat and major export commodity. It contributes 0.4 percent in GDP and 1.9 percent
value added in Agriculture. The production of Rice during this year estimated at 7.3 million
tonnes lower by 13.28 percent than last year's production of 8.4 million tonnes. The rice area
and production target for year 2022-23 were fixed at 3.06 million hectares and 8.45 million
tonnes respectively.

Maize:

         Maize is the third important cereal crop of Pakistan after wheat and rice, and it
contributes 0.7 percent to GDP. Maize is cultivated as a multipurpose crop for food, feed and
fodder. During 2022-23, maize was cultivated on an area of 1.7 million hectares showing an
increase of 4.1 percent over last year's 1.6 million hectares. Its production increased to 10.9
million tonnes witnessing significant growth of 6.9 percent over 9.5 million tonnes last year.
The increase in production was largely due to increase in area, availability of improved variety
of seed, and better economic returns.

Sugarcane:

         Sugarcane is a high value cash crop of Pakistan and is of great significance for sugar
related industries. It is cultivated mainly in Punjab, Sindh, and Khyber Pakhtunkhwa. During
2022-23, sugarcane was cropped on 1.3 million hectares recorded an increase of 4.7 percent
compared to last year's sown area of 1.2 million hectares. The sugarcane crop production
recorded at 87.9 million tonnes during 2022-23, down by 0.75 percent over last year (88.6
million tonnes). This decrease was mainly due to unfavorable weather conditions and non-
availability of quality inputs.

Cotton:

         Cotton crop often referred to as the "white gold" of the country. Cotton is a major source
of employment for millions of farmers and laborers, and it is a key raw material for the country's
textile industry and it contributes around 0.3 percent to GDP. During 2022-23, the cropped area
increased to 2.1 million hectares (10.6 percent) against last year's 1.9 million hectares. Cotton
production decrease to 4.9 million bales (41 percent) against last year's 8.3 million bales.

Oilseeds, a diverse group of crops that includes soybeans, rapeseeds, mustard, sesame, ground
nuts, sunflower, canola, and others, play a fundamental role in global agriculture and human
wellbeing. These seeds are rich sources of edible oils, proteins, and other valuable nutrients,
making them essential components of our diet. Edible oil is Pakistan's largest food import
commodity ranking third in the import list after petroleum products and machinery. Pakistan
is dependent upon other countries to fulfill more than 90% edible oil requirement and spending
a big amount of foreign exchange every year. The local production of edible oil is less than
10% of country's requirements. The gap between production and consumption of edible oil is
still increasing due to increase in population and increase in per capita consumption of edible
oil in our daily diet. In Pakistan, unlike other oilseed crops, the area under sesame increased by
30% from 199.89 thousand hectares in 2021-22 to 259.92 thousand hectares in 2022-23. Out
of the total area, 94.5% is in Punjab, 6.6% in Sindh, 3.1% in Balochistan, and 0.2% in
Balochistan. Production has also increased from 128.30 thousand tonnes in 2021-22 to 152.30
thousand tonnes in 2022.23. This may be due to the implementation of Oilseeds Enhancement
project.

In Pakistan, pulses are grown on 5% of the total cropped. The area under major pulses including
Gram, Mung, Mash and Masoor in the country is 1075.0 thousand hectares and the production
is around 526.8 thousand tonnes. Gram contributes 66%, Mung contributes 32%, Masoor
contributes 0.89% and Mash contributes 1.1% in the total production of pulses. The production
of pulses is far less than the requirement and the balance is met through imports. Major import
partner counties are Australia, Canada, Russia, Argentina, and Afghanistan.
